//! A session: the project, its file, and its undo history; commands run here.

use crate::json::Value;
use crate::project::Project;

pub struct Step {
    pub before: Project,
    pub after: Project,
    pub label: String,
}

pub struct Session {
    pub project: Project,
    pub path: Option<String>,
    pub undo: Vec<Step>,
    pub redo: Vec<Step>,
    pub read_only: bool,
    /// The phase and design option this session makes elements in.
    pub now_phase: Option<String>,
    pub now_option: Option<String>,
}

pub const MAX_UNDO: usize = 200;

impl Session {
    pub fn new() -> Session {
        Session {
            project: Project::new("en"),
            path: None,
            undo: Vec::new(),
            redo: Vec::new(),
            read_only: false,
            now_phase: None,
            now_option: None,
        }
    }

    /// Runs a command by id (or alias, any case) with its parameters.
    pub fn execute(&mut self, id: &str, params: &Value) -> Result<Value, String> {
        let Some((cid, label)) = crate::commands::resolve(id) else {
            return Err(format!("unknown command `{}`; list_commands shows them", id));
        };
        let before = self.project.clone();
        // the phase and option this session makes elements in go back with a refused command
        let now_before = (self.now_phase.clone(), self.now_option.clone());
        let res = crate::commands::run(self, &cid, params);
        match res {
            Ok(out) => {
                if crate::commands::changes_model(&cid) && !crate::commands::is_history_cmd(&cid) {
                    // a scan nothing places any more goes with the command that left it so
                    crate::cloud::drop_unplaced(&mut self.project);
                    // what a constrained dimension measures to follows what the command moved
                    if let Err(e) = crate::views::keep(&mut self.project) {
                        self.project = before;
                        (self.now_phase, self.now_option) = now_before;
                        return Err(e);
                    }
                    if let Err(e) = crate::check::check(&self.project) {
                        self.project = before;
                        (self.now_phase, self.now_option) = now_before;
                        return Err(e);
                    }
                    if self.project.to_value() != before.to_value() || crate::commands::always_step(&cid) {
                        self.undo.push(Step { before, after: self.project.clone(), label: label.clone() });
                        if self.undo.len() > MAX_UNDO {
                            self.undo.remove(0);
                        }
                        self.redo.clear();
                    }
                }
                Ok(out)
            }
            Err(e) => {
                self.project = before;
                (self.now_phase, self.now_option) = now_before;
                Err(e)
            }
        }
    }

    pub fn history(&self) -> Vec<String> {
        self.undo.iter().map(|s| s.label.clone()).collect()
    }
}
//! The model's rules, checked after every command that changes it.

use crate::project::Project;

pub fn check(pr: &Project) -> Result<(), String> {
    core_check(pr)?;
    crate::cloud::check(pr)?;
    crate::media::check(pr)?;
    crate::styles::check(pr)?;
    crate::arch::check(pr)?;
    crate::structure::check(pr)?;
    crate::views::check(pr)?;
    crate::edit::check(pr)?;
    crate::readers::check(pr)?;
    Ok(())
}

fn core_check(pr: &Project) -> Result<(), String> {
    let mut level_names: Vec<String> = Vec::new();
    let mut grid_names: Vec<String> = Vec::new();
    for (id, e) in &pr.elements {
        let cat = e.get("category").and_then(|c| c.as_str()).unwrap_or("");
        match cat {
            "level" => {
                let name = e.get("name").and_then(|n| n.as_str()).unwrap_or("").to_string();
                if level_names.contains(&name) {
                    return Err(format!("level {}: another level is already named `{}`", id, name));
                }
                level_names.push(name);
                if let Some(ext) = e.get("extent").and_then(|x| x.as_arr()) {
                    let a = ext.first().and_then(crate::commands::params::point_of);
                    let b = ext.get(1).and_then(crate::commands::params::point_of);
                    if let (Some(a), Some(b)) = (a, b) {
                        if !(b[0] - a[0] > 1.0 && b[1] - a[1] > 1.0) {
                            return Err(format!(
                                "level {}: its extent runs from its south-west corner to its north-east one",
                                id
                            ));
                        }
                    }
                }
            }
            "grid" => {
                let name = e.get("name").and_then(|n| n.as_str()).unwrap_or("").to_string();
                if grid_names.contains(&name) {
                    return Err(format!("grid {}: another grid is already named `{}`", id, name));
                }
                grid_names.push(name);
                let a = e.get("start").and_then(crate::commands::params::point_of).unwrap_or([0.0, 0.0]);
                let b = e.get("end").and_then(crate::commands::params::point_of).unwrap_or([0.0, 0.0]);
                let len = ((b[0] - a[0]).powi(2) + (b[1] - a[1]).powi(2)).sqrt();
                if len <= 1.0 {
                    return Err(format!("grid {}: too short ({} mm)", id, len));
                }
            }
            _ => {}
        }
    }
    Ok(())
}
//! Reading the model: `inspect` and `elements`.

use crate::json::{Map, Value};
use crate::session::Session;

pub fn inspect(s: &Session) -> Value {
    let pr = &s.project;
    let mut m = Map::new();
    let mut counts = Map::new();
    for e in pr.elements.values() {
        let c = e.get("category").and_then(|c| c.as_str()).unwrap_or("").to_string();
        let n = counts.get(&c).and_then(|v| v.as_u64()).unwrap_or(0);
        counts.insert(&c, Value::u(n + 1));
    }
    m.insert("counts", Value::Obj(counts));
    m.insert("history", s.history().into());
    m.insert("language", pr.language().into());
    m.insert("name", pr.name().into());
    let levels: Vec<Value> = crate::commands::basic::level_ids_sorted(pr)
        .iter()
        .map(|id| {
            let e = pr.el(*id).unwrap();
            jobj! {"elevation" => e.get("elevation").cloned().unwrap_or(Value::Null), "id" => *id, "name" => e.get("name").cloned().unwrap_or(Value::Null)}
        })
        .collect();
    m.insert("levels", Value::Arr(levels));
    for (k, v) in crate::styles::inspect_entries(s) {
        m.insert(&k, v);
    }
    let mut views = Vec::new();
    crate::views::inspect_entries(s, &mut views);
    for (k, v) in views {
        m.insert(&k, v);
    }
    Value::Obj(m)
}

pub fn elements(s: &Session, p: &Value) -> Result<Value, String> {
    let pr = &s.project;
    let cat = p.get("category").and_then(|c| c.as_str()).map(|c| c.to_string());
    let mut out = Vec::new();
    for (id, e) in &pr.elements {
        let c = e.get("category").and_then(|c| c.as_str()).unwrap_or("");
        if let Some(want) = &cat {
            if c != want {
                continue;
            }
        }
        let mut x = e.clone();
        x.set("id", Value::u(*id));
        if c == "level" {
            x.set("computation_height", Value::f(1200.0));
        }
        if c == "pointcloud" {
            crate::cloud::element_view(pr, &mut x);
        }
        // what the views and the annotation show beyond their fields
        crate::views::element_view(pr, *id, &mut x);
        out.push(x);
    }
    let n = out.len();
    Ok(jobj! {"count" => n, "elements" => Value::Arr(out), "returned" => n})
}

Its forms are profiles drawn in its own axes (x along a component's angle, y to its left, z up from its base) and swept: `xy` up from `from` to `to`, `xz` along y, `yz` along x. A `void` is no solid: it cuts the solid forms listed before it that it passes through, a hole, a notch, a sink's bowl, and is never seen itself (forms listed after it are whole). The head loss is Darcy-Weisbach (Colebrook, PVC at 20 °C) over `length` (mm) plus the equivalent length of the `fittings`; with `pressure` (kPa, dynamic, upstream) and `rise` (mm, up) it replies the pressure left at the end, 10 kPa at least at a point of use, and `ok` covers both; without `pressure` `ok` is the speed alone (`ok_covers` says which). It has no shortcut: Revit has no tool for it. It changes nothing"},{"aliases":[],"id":"storm_size","label":"Size a Storm Drain","menu":null,"params":"{kind: downpipe|horizontal, area?, rainfall?, flow?, outlet?: edge|funnel, gutter?, length?, slope?, inside?} a storm drain as NBR 10844 asks. The flow is `flow` (L/min), or the rain on `area` (m², as the standard counts a roof) at `rainfall` (mm/h; 150 by default, which the standard allows for up to 100 m² only: above that `rainfall` is required): Q = I·A/60. A `downpipe` (condutor vertical) takes the inner diameter of the standard's Figure 3 abacuses by Q, the water depth in the gutter `gutter` (50 to 100 mm), its `length` (mm, 0.3 m or more) and the gutter's `outlet` (edge: sharp-edged, the default; funnel); 70 mm inside at least. A `horizontal` drain takes Manning at two thirds full (n 0.011, PVC only, from the standard's Table 2) at its `slope` (a fraction, 0.005 at least): the inner diameter it needs, and with `inside` (mm) whether that pipe carries the flow. Off the abacuses (more than 150 mm inside) nothing is read. A key that does not apply to the `kind`, or `flow` together with `area`, is an error. It has no shortcut: Revit has no tool for it. It changes nothing"},{"aliases":[],"id":"library","label":"Library","menu":null,"params":"{kind?, name?} the project's library with its ids and GUIDs, as elements have them: each type (`kind` `type:wall`, `type:door`, `type:beam`…), material, picture, family, parameter, view filter and template (`view-filter`, `view-template`), phase, phase filter, design option set and design option (`option-set`, `design-option`, named `set: option`), family type and family parameter (`family-type`, `family-parameter`, named `family: name`); and `parts`, what entries hold that has its own id and GUID: each type's layers and each family's forms and symbols (`kind` `layer`, `form`, `symbol`; `name` the type's or the family's). Wherever a command takes an entry by name, its GUID does too"},{"aliases":[],"id":"select_by_id","label":"Select by ID","menu":"Manage > Select by ID","params":"{text} the elements a list names, each by its id, its GUID (dashes and braces or not) or its IFC GlobalId, separated by spaces, commas, semicolons or lines: `ids` found, each once in the order named, `missing` (what named none), and `plan` and `extent` ([[x, y], [x, y]] mm), the level's plan that shows them and where; it selects nothing itself, a front end does"},{"aliases":[],"id":"ids_of","label":"IDs of Selection","menu":"Manage > IDs of Selection","params":"{ids: [id or GUID, …]} what names each element: its `id`, its `guid`, its `ifc` GlobalId, its `category` and its `name` (or type, number, mark)"},{"aliases":[],"id":"filter_selection","label":"Filter","menu":null,"params":"{ids: [id or GUID, …], by?, keep?: [value, …]} Revit's Filter on a selection: `groups` [{value, count, ids}, …], the values `by` takes among the elements (by default `Category`, whose values are categories' keys: wall, door, detail_line…; else a parameter as `fields` lists them, values as schedules show them, `\"\"` for none), and `fields`, the parameters every one of them has, to filter by. With `keep`, `ids`: those holding one of its values, the selection to keep; it selects nothing itself, a front end does"},{"aliases":[],"id":"elements","label":"Elements","menu":null,"params":"{category?, level?, limit?, offset?} elements with their `id` (six digits, what commands take) and `guid` (made once, never changed), fields and derived values (length, height, area_m2…); what an element points at (its level, host, view…) it holds by GUID. Wherever a command takes an element, its id or its GUID will do"}]"##;

pub const USAGE: &str = r##"USAGE:
  bimcraft-cli run [FILE] [--sample | --highrise] [--cmd 'ID {json}']… [--script FILE]… [--save PATH]…
      Open FILE (a .bimcraft project, or an .ifc or Revit .rvt file to import) or start empty, then in order: build the sample house, run
      commands and scripts (one `ID {json}` per line), save. --save writes by extension:
      .bimcraft (the project), .ifc (IFC4), .glb (the 3D view's scene as glTF), .svg or .png (the lowest level's plan).
      --highrise builds an original 20-storey performance sample (about 10,000 elements).
  bimcraft-cli info FILE         levels, element counts, types and warnings of a project or an IFC file; of a Revit .rvt or
                                 .rfa file, its release, last save, levels and element counts by category
  bimcraft-cli convert IN OUT    a project, an IFC file or a Revit .rvt file to .ifc, .glb, .svg, .png or .bimcraft
  bimcraft-cli commands          every command and its parameters
  bimcraft-cli mcp [--read-only] [FILE | --connect [PORT]]
                                 an MCP server on stdio, for agents: on a headless project of its own (FILE, opened first, or an
                                 empty one), or with --connect on the project open in a window whose control port is open
                                 (`bimcraft --control PORT` or File > Settings > Agents; without PORT, the last one). With
                                 --read-only the agent may read the project, never change it or write over its file
  bimcraft-cli --version         the release
"##;
//! Reading a project file into a project.

use crate::json::Value;
use crate::project::Project;

pub fn load(v: Value) -> Result<Project, String> {
    if !matches!(v, Value::Obj(_)) {
        return Err(format!("invalid type: {}, expected struct ProjectFile", v.kind_name()));
    }
    let mut p = Project::from_value(v);
    crate::cloud::load(&mut p)?;
    Ok(p)
}
